Two endpoints matter:\n\n- **`POST /skServer/validateBackup`** — the configuration-archive **import**. It accepts a multipart upload of a `.backup` zip (filename must match `signalk-*.backup`), unzips it into a server-side temp dir, and replies with the list of restorable config files. In `setupApp` (bundle offset ~5405961) the `/restore` path gets `adminAuthenticationMiddleware`, but **`/validateBackup` is not in any middleware list** — any unauthenticated caller can push config content into the server's restore staging area.\n- **`POST /skServer/restore`** — the **apply** step. It copies the staged files (`settings.json`, `defaults.json`, `baseDeltas.json`, `security.json`, `package.json`) into `SIGNALK_NODE_CONFIG_DIR` and returns `202`. Correctly admin-gated: 401 without a token.\n\nSo an unauthenticated import + an authenticated apply (token from `POST /login` with the `ADMINUSER` credentials the script provisions itself) lands attacker-controlled content in the server's configuration storage.\n\n## What the script does\n\n1. Picks a free loopback port, generates its own admin credentials, launches the binary with `PORT`, `ADMINUSER`, `SIGNALK_NODE_CONFIG_DIR=<fresh tmpdir>`, and pumps the child's merged output for a log tail.\n2. Probes `/skServer/loginStatus` to discover the route prefix, probes for the login/import/apply endpoints (no hardcoded assumption that `/skServer` exists — falls back to root-prefixed paths).\n3. Builds the zip archive **at runtime** (`settings.json`, `defaults.json`, `baseDeltas.json`, each embedding a unique marker) plus the multipart body — nothing read from disk.\n4. Sends the import **with no `Authorization` header, no cookie, no prior session**. If rejected, it records the status and does *not* retry with credentials.\n5. Logs in via `POST /login` to get a JWT, sends `Authorization: JWT <token>` on the apply request.\n6. Polls the config dir for the marker, reads the affected file's content, and emits the JSON contract.\n\n## Verified outcomes\n\n| Build | `triggered` | `import_http` | `apply_http` | `config_replaced` | `error` |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Real target (×3 runs) | `true` | 200 | 202 | `true` | `null` |\n| Fake server rejecting unauth import | `false` | 401 | 401 | `false` | `null` |\n| Nonexistent binary | — | — | — | — | `\"failed to start binary: …\"` |\n\nOn the real target, `config_content` comes back as the marker-stamped file written into the config dir, and `returncode`/`signal` are `-15`/`SIGTERM` because the script terminates the still-running child.
